Convergence of boundary layers of chemotaxis models with physical boundary conditions II: Non-degenerate initial data Thanks: ∗ Corresponding author.
Guangyi Hong, Zhi-an Wang ∗ \ast

Abstract
. This paper establishes the convergence of boundary-layer solutions of the consumption type Keller-Segel model with non-degenerate initial data subject to physical boundary conditions, which is a sequel of [ 5 ] on the case of degenerate initial data. Specifically, we justify that the solution with positive chemical diffusion rate ε > 0 \varepsilon>0 converges to the solution with zero diffusion ε = 0 \varepsilon=0 (outer-layer solution) plus the boundary-layer profiles (inner-layer solution) for any time t > 0 t>0 as ε → 0 \varepsilon\rightarrow 0 . Compared to [ 5 ] , the main difficulty in the analysis is the lack of regularity of the outer- and boundary-layer profiles since only the zero-order compatibility conditions for the leading-order boundary-layer profiles can be fulfilled with non-degenerate initial data. Our new strategy is to regularize the boundary-layer profiles with carefully designed corner-corrector functions and approximate the low-regularity leading-order boundary-layer profiles by higher-regularity profiles with regularized boundary conditions. By using delicate weight functions involving boundary-layer profiles to cancel the multi-scaled linear terms in the perturbed equations, we manage to obtain the requisite uniform-in- ε \varepsilon estimates for the convergence analysis. This cancellation technique enables us to prove the convergence to boundary-layer solutions for any time t > 0 t>0 , which is different from the convergence result in [ 5 ] which holds true only for some finite time depending on the Dirichlet boundary value. 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification. 35B40, 35K61, 35Q92, 76D10 Keywords. Boundary layers; chemotaxis; linear sensitivity; corner-corrector functions.



1.
Introduction

This paper is concerned with the following Keller-Segel chemotaxis system

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle u_{t}=\Delta u-\nabla\cdot(u\nabla\phi(v))&\mbox{in }\Omega,\\
\displaystyle v_{t}=\varepsilon\Delta v-uv&\mbox{in }\Omega,\end{cases}

(1.1)

where
u(x,t)
and
v(x,t)
denote the cell density and chemical (signal) concentration, respectively.
\varepsilon>0
represent the chemical diffusion. The function
\phi(v)
is referred to as the chemotactic sensitivity function accounting for the signal transduction mechanism with two prevailing prototypes:
\phi(v)=v
(linear sensitivity) and
\phi(v)=\log v
(logarithmic sensitivity).
The chemotaxis model (
1.1
) with logarithmic sensitivity was first proposed
[
20
]
to explain the propagation of traveling bands driven by the bacterial chemotaxis observed in the celebrated experiment of Adler
[
1
]
. Since then, a large number of mathematical works have been developed based on this model, such as existence and stability of traveling wave solutions
[
19
,
33
,
42
,
28
,
25
,
31
,
4
,
10
,
8
]
, global well-posedness
[
27
,
40
,
29
]
, boundary-layer solutions
[
6
,
24
,
18
,
16
,
17
]
, just to mention a few. In contrast, the results on (
1.1
) with linear sensitivity are much less. The early results are mainly restricted to global well-posedness of solutions (existence and stability) in bounded domains with Neumann boundary conditions (cf.
[
12
,
36
,
37
]
).

Among other things, this paper is concerned with (
1.1
) subject to the following physical boundary conditions

\displaystyle\displaystyle\partial_{\nu}u-u\partial_{\nu}v=0,\ \ v=v_{\ast}\ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \partial\Omega,

(1.2)

where
\nu
denotes the unit outward normal vector of the boundary
\partial\Omega
, and
v_{*}>0
is a constant. That is, the zero-flux boundary condition is imposed to the bacterial density while Dirichlet boundary condition to the chemical concentration. The boundary conditions (
1.2
) first appeared in
[
39
]
for a chemotaxis-fluid system describing the boundary accumulation layer formed on the drop edge (air-water interface) in a sessile drop mixed with aerobic bacterial
Bacillus ubtilis
undertaking chemotactic movement.
When
\phi(v)=v
, the existence of unique non-constant stationary boundary-layer solution to (
1.1
)-(
1.2
) was proved in
[
22
]
in all dimensions, followed by the stability results in one dimension
[
15
]
and in multi-dimensions
[
26
]
. The existence of classical solutions in two-dimensional radially symmetric domain and weak solutions or small-data classical solutions in higher dimensions were proved in
[
21
,
41
]
. We also mention another result in
[
2
]
on the existence of stationary solutions for (
1.1
) with linear sensitivity and a Robin type condition for
v
based on Henry’s law modeling the dissolution of gas in water. When
\phi(v)=\log v
, the existence and stability of the non-constant stationary solution (
1.1
)-(
1.2
) was obtained in
[
6
]
followed by a work
[
35
]
further giving the stabilization rate. Recently the existence of unique stationary solution of (
1.1
)-(
1.2
) in all dimensions for any
\varepsilon>0
and asymptotic profile of boundary-layer solutions as
\varepsilon\to 0
were established in
[
7
]
.

The unique stationary solution of (
1.1
)-(
1.2
) with
\phi(v)=v
was shown to be a boundary-layer profile as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
with thickness of order
\sqrt{\varepsilon}
in
[
22
]
, which seemed to be the first rigorous result justifying the existence of boundary-layer solutions for chemotaxis models and supported the experimental findings reported in
[
39
]
in the absence of fluid dynamics apart from the numerical simulations in
[
39
,
9
,
23
]
.
However, except the results in
[
22
,
15
,
26
]
on the stationary problem, the analytical results on the boundary-layer solutions of the time-dependent problem (
1.1
)-(
1.2
) are largely open. In particular, whether the solution of (
1.1
)-(
1.2
) converges as
\varepsilon\to 0
remains poorly understood. Recently, the model (
1.1
)-(
1.2
) with linear sensitivity in a bounded interval
\mathcal{I}:=(0,1)
was considered in
[
5
]
:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle u_{t}=u_{xx}-\left(uv_{x}\right)_{x},&\ x\in\mathcal{I},\ t>0,\\
\displaystyle v_{t}=\varepsilon v_{xx}-uv,&\ x\in\mathcal{I},\ t>0,\\
(u,v)(x,0)=(u_{0},v_{0})(x),&\ x\in\overline{\mathcal{I}},\end{cases}

(1.3)

with boundary conditions

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(u_{x}-uv_{x})|_{\partial\mathcal{I}}=0,\ \ v|_{\partial\mathcal{I}}=v_{\ast},&\text{if}\ \varepsilon>0,\\
(u_{x}-uv_{x})|_{\partial\mathcal{I}}=0,&\text{if}\ \varepsilon=0,\end{cases}

(1.4)

where
\overline{\mathcal{I}}=[0,1]
and
\partial\mathcal{I}=\{0,1\}
. One may intuitively suspect that the boundary-layer profile may arise from the problem (
1.3
)-(
1.4
) as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Indeed, when
\varepsilon=0
, the boundary value of
v
is solely determined by directly solving the ODE
v_{t}=-uv
of the second equation of (
1.3
):

v|_{\partial\mathcal{I}}=v_{0}|_{\partial\mathcal{I}}\ {\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\int_{0}^{t}u|_{\partial\mathcal{I}}\mathrm{d}\tau}.

(1.5)

This boundary value of
v
may mismatch the one prescribed in (
1.4
) for
\varepsilon>0
. If this occurs, boundary layers will usually develop and consequently
(u^{\varepsilon},v^{\varepsilon})\nrightarrow(u^{0},v^{0})
in
L^{\infty}(\mathcal{I})
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
(u^{\varepsilon},v^{\varepsilon})
and
(u^{0},v^{0})
denote the solution of (
1.3
)-(
1.4
) with
\varepsilon>0
and
\varepsilon=0
, respectively. Then an immediate question is under what conditions the boundary layer will be present and what is the limit of
(u^{\varepsilon},v^{\varepsilon})
as
\varepsilon\to 0
then.
In
[
5
]
, we find proper compatibility conditions warranting the development of boundary-layer solutions as
\varepsilon\to 0
and further show that the solution of (
1.3
)-(
1.4
) with
\varepsilon>0
converges to
(u^{0},v^{0})
plus the boundary-layer solutions as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. However, the results of
[
5
]
have two essential confinements. One is that the analyses in
[
5
]
heavily rely on the assumption
u_{0}|_{\partial\mathcal{I}}=0
, namely the initial value
u_{0}
is degenerate at the boundary. The other is the results of
[
5
]
hold only for
t\in(0,T_{0}(v_{*}))
for a finite time
T_{0}(v_{*})
depending on
v_{\ast}>0
. This paper is a sequel to
[
5
]
by studying the case of non-degenerate initial data (i.e.
\inf_{x\in\overline{\mathcal{I}}}u_{0}>0
). In this case we show that the solution of (
1.3
)-(
1.4
) with
\varepsilon>0
converges to
(u^{0},v^{0})
plus the boundary-layer solutions as
\varepsilon\to 0
for any
t>0
irrespective of
v_{*}>0
.

The main challenges in studying the problem (
1.3
)-(
1.4
) come from the cross-diffusion (chemotaxis) term
(uv_{x})_{x}
and the complex physical boundary conditions. To deal with the cross-diffusion, the previous works
[
12
,
36
]
concerning the global well-posedness of solutions with homogeneous Neumann boundary conditions rely on the construction of a Lyapunov functional. With the physical boundary conditions in (
1.4
), the work
[
15
]
cleverly identified a cancellation structure by eliminating the cross-diffusion with the technique of taking anti-derivatives in one dimension and established the time-asymptotic stability of the stationary boundary-layer profile. For the convergence of boundary-layer profiles as
\varepsilon\to 0
, the analyses of
[
5
]
need the degeneracy of
u_{0}
to meet the compatibility conditions. However, this degeneracy incapacitates cancellation technique of
[
15
]
for the singular linear term. Alternatively, the Hardy inequality was utilized in
[
5
]
to derive the requisite estimates for convergence analyses as
\varepsilon\to 0
, but this convergence result based on the estimates derived by the Hardy inequality holds up to a finite time depending on
v_{*}>0
as mentioned above. For the non-degenerate initial data considered in this paper, the cancellation technique can be used but the compatibility conditions can only be fulfilled at the leading-order boundary-layer profiles. This confines boundary-layer profiles to have low temporal regularity only which is inadequate for the convergence analysis. To enhance the regularity, we introduce corner-correction functions into the boundary conditions for the leading boundary-layer profile in this paper to meet the full compatibility conditions, see (
2.12
) and (
2.18
). But introducing these functions raises new challenges to derive the uniform-in-
\varepsilon
estimates of solutions needed for the convergence analysis, since the time derivative of the corner-correction function is singular in
\varepsilon
. To overcome this adverse effect caused by the corner-correction functions, we employ various time-weighted functions to derive the requisite uniform-in-
\varepsilon
estimates by requiring all the higher-order estimates of solutions be away from the initial time to avoid the possible initial layers (see estimates in Sec. 3). The advantage of non-degenerate initial data is that we can use cancellation technique instead of the Hardy inequality to deal with the singular linear terms and manage to establish necessary uniform-in-
\varepsilon
estimates (see the proof of Lemma
4.4
) so that convergence result holds for all time
t>0
, different from the convergence result in
[
5
]
which holds for
t\in[0,T_{0}(v_{*})]
for some finite time
T_{0}(v_{*})
depending on
v_{*}
. This is a major difference from the degenerate initial data considered in
[
5
]
. But our current convergence result can not cover the time
t=0
, which is a price paid to the case of non-degenerate initial data.

The rest of the paper is organized as follows: In Sec.
2
, we first introduce the equations for the regularized outer- and boundary-layer profiles with corner-correction functions, and then state our main results on the convergence of solutions of (
1.3
)-(
1.4
) as
\varepsilon\to 0
. In Sec.
3
, we study the regularity and the dependence on
\varepsilon
of the regularized outer- and boundary-layer profiles. Finally, in Sec.
4
, we show the convergence of boundary-layer solutions and prove our main results.

2.
Statement of main results

In this section, we first introduce the equations for the regularized outer- and boundary-layer profiles, and then state our main results on the convergence of boundary-layer solutions as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. For clarity, we highlight some notations used throughout the paper.

Notation.

•

Denote
\bar{\mathcal{I}}=[0,1]
,
\mathbb{R}_{+}:=(0,\infty)
and
\mathbb{R}_{-}:=(-\infty,0)
.
\mathbb{N}
represents the set of non-negative integers. Let
L^{p}
with
1\leq p\leq\infty
represent the Lebesgue space
L^{p}(\mathcal{I})
in which functions are defined with respect to (w.r.t.) the variable
x\in(0,1)
.
L_{z}^{p}
denotes the space
L^{p}((0,\infty))
for functions defined w.r.t.
z\in(0,\infty)
and
L_{\xi}^{p}
denotes
L^{p}((0,\infty))
for functions defined w.r.t.
\xi\in(-\infty,0)
. Similarly, we denote by
H^{k}
,
H_{z}^{k}
and
H_{\xi}^{k}
the standard Sobolev spaces
W^{k,2}
for functions defined w.r.t.
x\in\mathcal{I}
,
z\in(0,\infty)
and
\xi\in(-\infty,0)
, respectively. We also write
L_{T}^{p}Y:=L^{p}(0,T;Y)
(e.g.,
L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{\infty}:=L^{\infty}(0,T;L_{z}^{\infty})
) without confusion caused.

•

Denote
\langle z\rangle=\sqrt{1+z^{2}}
for
z\in[0,\infty)
, and
\langle\xi\rangle=\sqrt{1+\xi^{2}}
for
\xi\in(-\infty,0]
.

•

Denote by
C
a generic positive constant which may depend on the time variable but independent of
\varepsilon
, and may vary in the context.

•

We often use
(\ast)_{i}
to denote the
i
-th equation of the system
(\ast)
for brevity.

2.1.
Construction of outer-/boundary-layer profiles

In this subsection, we shall first reformulate the problem (
1.3
) and then present the equations for the (regularized) outer- and boundary-layer profiles. Notice that the zero-flux boundary condition for
u
immediately yields the conservation of mass. That is,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{I}}u(x,t)\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathcal{I}}u_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x.

Denote
M:=\int_{\mathcal{I}}u_{0}\mathrm{d}x
, and take

\displaystyle\displaystyle\varphi=\int_{0}^{x}(u-M)\mathrm{d}y\ \ \mbox{with}\ \ \varphi(x,0)=\int_{0}^{x}(u_{0}-M)\mathrm{d}y=:\varphi_{0}(x).

(2.1)

Then the problem (
1.3
) can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\makebox[-3.0pt]{~}\varphi_{t}=\varphi_{xx}-(\varphi_{x}+M)v_{x},

(2.2a)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\makebox[-3.0pt]{~}v_{t}=\varepsilon v_{xx}-(\varphi_{x}+M)v,

(2.2b)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\makebox[-3.0pt]{~}\varphi(0,t)=\varphi(1,t)=0,\ \ v(0,t)=v(1,t)=v_{\ast},

(2.2c)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\makebox[-3.0pt]{~}(\varphi,v)(x,0)=(\varphi_{0},v_{0}).

(2.2d)

With the transformation (
2.1
) motivated by the mass conservation of
u
, we define
\varphi
as the primitive function of
u-M
and convert the original cross-diffusion problem (
1.3
) into the problem (
2.2
) without cross-diffusion for which the Dirichlet boundary conditions can be fully used. As mentioned before, when
\varepsilon=0
,
v
is determined by (
1.5
), one can not impose boundary conditions for
v
to avoid overdetermination. Therefore the boundary condition for the case
\varepsilon=0
reads:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\varphi(0,t)=\varphi(1,t)=0.

We proceed to present the equations for the outer- and boundary-layer profiles of the problem (
2.2
) with small
\varepsilon>0
which are precisely derived in our previous work
[
5
]
based on the perturbation method (cf.
[
13
,
14
,
32
]
). We assume that the solutions of the transformed problem (
2.2
) with
\varepsilon>0
have expansions in the form:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\varphi^{\varepsilon}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{\frac{j}{2}}\left(\varphi^{I,j}(x,t)+\varphi^{B,j}\left(z,t\right)+\varphi^{b,j}\left(\xi,t\right)\right),

(2.3a)

\displaystyle\displaystyle v^{\varepsilon}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{\frac{j}{2}}\left(v^{I,j}(x,t)+v^{B,j}\left(z,t\right)+v^{b,j}\left(\xi,t\right)\right)

(2.3b)

for
j\in\mathbb{N}
, where
z
and
\xi
are called the boundary-layer coordinates defined by

\displaystyle\displaystyle z=\frac{x}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}},\ \ \ \xi=\frac{x-1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}},\ \ x\in[0,1],

(2.4)

with
\sqrt{\varepsilon}
referred to as the boundary-layer thickness which can be formally determined by the balancing argument (cf.
[
14
,
18
]
). Furthermore, the boundary-layer profiles
(\varphi^{B,j},v^{B,j})
and
(\varphi^{b,j},v^{b,j})
are assumed to be smooth and possess the following property for
j\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mbox{$\varphi^{B,j}$ and $v^{B,j}$ decay to zero rapidly as $z\rightarrow\infty$,}\\
\mbox{while $\varphi^{b,j}$ and $v^{b,j}$ decay to zero rapidly as $\xi\rightarrow-\infty$.}\end{cases}

(2.5)

To match the boundary conditions, we assume that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\varphi^{I,j}(0,t)+\varphi^{B,j}(0,t)=0,\ \ \varphi^{I,j}(1,t)+\varphi^{b,j}(0,t)=0,\ \ \ j\geq 0,\\
\displaystyle v^{I,0}(0,t)+v^{B,0}(0,t)=v_{\ast},\ \ v^{I,0}(1,t)+v^{b,0}(0,t)=v_{\ast},\\
\displaystyle v^{I,j}(0,t)+v^{B,j}(0,t)=0,\ \ v^{I,j}(1,t)+v^{b,j}(0,t)=0,\ \ j\geq 1,\end{cases}

where we have neglected
(\varphi^{b,j}(-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{1/2}},t),v^{b,j}(-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{1/2}},t))
at
x=0
and
(\varphi^{B,j}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{1/2}},t),v^{B,j}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{1/2}},t))
at
x=1
based on the decay properties in (
2.5
) since
\varepsilon
is small. For the initial data, we assume that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\varphi^{I,0}(x,0)=\varphi_{0}(x),\ \ \varphi^{B,0}(z,0)=\varphi^{b,0}(\xi,0)=0,\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle v^{I,0}(x,0)=v_{0}(x),\ \ v^{B,0}(z,0)=v^{b,0}(\xi,0)=0,\end{cases}

and that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\varphi^{I,j}(x,0)=\varphi^{B,j}(z,0)=\varphi^{b,j}(\xi,0)=0,\ \ j\geq 1,\\
\displaystyle v^{I,j}(x,0)=v^{B,j}(z,0)=v^{b,j}(\xi,0)=0,\ \ j\geq 1.\end{cases}

Substituting the ansatz in (
2.3
) into the equations in (
2.2
), one can get the equations for the outer-/boundary-layer profiles for
j\geq 0
. For details, please see
[
5
]
. We now quote some equations governing the outer-/boundary-layer profiles derived in
[
5
]
for later use. The leading-order outer-layer profile
(\varphi^{I,0},v^{I,0})
satisfies the problem

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\varphi_{t}^{I,0}=\varphi_{xx}^{I,0}-(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}+M)v_{x}^{I,0},&x\in\mathcal{I},\ t>0,\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle v_{t}^{I,0}=-(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}+M)v^{I,0},&x\in\mathcal{I},\ t>0,\\[2.84526pt]
\varphi^{I,0}(0,t)=\varphi^{I,0}(1,t)=0,\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle(\varphi^{I,0},v^{I,0})(x,0)=(\varphi_{0},v_{0}),&x\in\bar{\mathcal{I}},\end{cases}

(2.6)

which is the zero-diffusion problem of (
2.2
). As shown in
[
5
]
, this problem admits a unique global classical solution if the initial value is compatible with boundary conditions and smooth enough. Now let us turn to the equations describing the leading-order boundary-layer profile. The boundary-layer profile
\varphi^{B,0}
near the left boundary satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\varphi_{zz}^{B,0}-\varphi_{z}^{B,0}v_{z}^{B,0}=0,\ \ z\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\\[2.84526pt]
\varphi^{B,0}(0,t)=0,\ \ \varphi^{B,0}(+\infty,t)=0,\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle\varphi^{B,0}(x,0)=0,\end{cases}

which along with (
2.5
) gives
\varphi^{B,0}\equiv 0
. The boundary-layer profile
v^{B,0}
near the left boundary solves

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle v_{t}^{B,0}=v^{B,0}_{zz}-(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M)v^{I,0}(0,t)({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,0}}-1)-(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M){\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,0}}v^{B,0},\ \ z\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle v^{B,0}(0,t)=v_{\ast}-v^{I,0}(0,t),\ \ v^{B,0}(+\infty,t)=0,\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle v^{B,0}(z,0)=0,\end{cases}

(2.7)

and the first-order left boundary-layer profile
\varphi^{B,1}
is determined by
v^{B,0}
through

\displaystyle\displaystyle\varphi^{B,1}=-\int_{z}^{\infty}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M)\left({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,0}(y,t)}-1\right)\mathrm{d}y.

(2.8)

The right boundary-layer profile
v^{b,0}
satisfies

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle v_{t}^{b,0}=v^{b,0}_{\xi\xi}-(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(1,t)+M)v^{I,0}(1,t)({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{b,0}}-1)-(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(1,t)+M){\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{b,0}}v^{b,0},\ \ \xi\in\mathbb{R}_{-},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle v^{b,0}(0,t)=v_{\ast}-v^{I,0}(1,t),\ \ v^{b,0}(-\infty,t)=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle v^{b,0}(\xi,0)=0.\end{cases}

(2.9)

Furthermore, we have
\varphi^{b,0}\equiv 0
, and
\varphi^{b,1}
is given by

\displaystyle\displaystyle\varphi^{b,1}=\int_{-\infty}^{\xi}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(1,t)+M)\left({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{b,0}(y,t)}-1\right)\mathrm{d}y.

(2.10)

To obtain the existence and regularity of solutions to problems (
2.7
) and (
2.9
), it is natural to assume
v_{0}|_{\partial\mathcal{I}}=v|_{\partial\mathcal{I}}=v_{\ast}
. Under the scenario
\displaystyle\min_{x\in\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(\varphi_{0x}+M)>0\,(\mbox{i.e.},\displaystyle\min_{x\in\bar{\mathcal{I}}}u_{0}>0)
considered in this paper, one can check that the initial data of (
2.7
) are compatible with boundary conditions at the zero-order only
(i.e.
v^{B,0}|_{t=0}(0)=v^{B,0}(0,t)|_{t=0}=0
), but not at the higher order terms since

[\partial_{t}^{k}v^{B,0}|_{t=0}](0)\neq\partial_{t}^{k}v^{B,0}(0,t)|_{t=0}\ \text{for}\ k\geq 1.

This applies to the right boundary-layer profile
v^{b,0}
of (
2.9
) as well. Consequently the solutions to the problem (
2.7
) (resp. (
2.9
)) would not be smooth enough at the corner
(0,0)
in the
z
-
t
(resp.
\xi
-
t
) plane, which may give rise to weak initial layers. To avoid excessive analysis on initial layers and to circumvent the difficulty caused by the lack of regularity of the boundary-layer profiles at the corner, we use a function
v^{B,\varepsilon}
to approximate
v^{B,0}
by modifying the boundary conditions for
v^{B,0}
with a time-dependent corner-corrector function. Specifically,
v^{B,\varepsilon}
solves

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle v_{t}^{B,\varepsilon}=v^{B,\varepsilon}_{zz}-(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M)v^{I,0}(0,t)({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}-1)-(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M){\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}v^{B,\varepsilon},\ \ z\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle v^{B,\varepsilon}(0,t)=v_{\ast}-v^{I,0}(0,t)+\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t),\ \ v^{B,\varepsilon}(+\infty,t)=0,\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle v^{B,\varepsilon}(z,0)=0,\end{cases}

(2.11)

where
\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)
is a corner-corrector function defined by

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=-2\int_{0}^{t}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,s)+M)v^{I,0}(0,s)\int_{s}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\quad-2(\varphi_{0x}(0)+M)v_{\ast}\int_{0}^{t}\chi(\frac{\tau}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}})\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}\tau,

(2.12)

with
\alpha>0
being a constant such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle 1<\alpha<\frac{5}{4},

(2.13)

and
\chi(s)
being a smooth function defined on
[0,\infty)
such that
\chi(0)=1
and
\chi(s)=0
for
s\geq 1
. For some properties on the corner-corrector
\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)
, please see Lemma
3.1
. Clearly, if we assume
v_{0}|_{\partial\mathcal{I}}=v_{\ast}
, the initial data for the approximate problem (
2.11
) are compatible with boundary conditions up to order two. That is,
v^{B,\varepsilon}(0,t)|_{t=0}=0
, and
\partial_{t}^{k}v^{B,\varepsilon}|_{t=0}\,(k=1,2)
defined by the initial data through
\eqref{first-bd-layer-pro-appro}_{1}
satisfy
[\partial_{t}^{k}v^{B,\varepsilon}|_{t=0}](0)=\partial_{t}^{k}v^{B,\varepsilon}(0,t)|_{t=0}
. Furthermore,
v^{B,\varepsilon}
is a good approximation of
v^{B,0}
in the sense that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Big\|(v^{B,\varepsilon}-v^{B,0})\Big(\frac{x}{\varepsilon^{1/2}},t\Big)\Big\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L^{\infty}}\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha}{2}},

(2.14)

for details, please see Appendix
B
. With
v^{B,\varepsilon}
, we can define the approximation of
\varphi^{B,1}
as follows

\displaystyle\displaystyle\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}=-\int_{z}^{\infty}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M)\left({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}(y,t)}-1\right)\mathrm{d}y.

(2.15)

By (
2.8
), (
2.14
), (
2.15
) and (
3.8
), one can deduce that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Big\|\partial_{x}^{k}(\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}-\varphi^{B,1})\Big(\frac{x}{\varepsilon^{1/2}},t\Big)\Big\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L^{\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha}{2}},\ \ k=0,1.

(2.16)

Similarly, we approximate
(v^{b,0},\varphi^{b,0})
by
(v^{b,\varepsilon},\varphi^{b,\varepsilon})
which satisfies

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle v_{t}^{b,\varepsilon}=v^{b,\varepsilon}_{\xi\xi}-(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(1,t)+M)v^{I,0}(1,t)({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{b,\varepsilon}}-1)-(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(1,t)+M){\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{b,\varepsilon}}v^{b,\varepsilon},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\varphi^{b,\varepsilon}=\int_{-\infty}^{\xi}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(1,t)+M)\left({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{b,\varepsilon}(y,t)}-1\right)\mathrm{d}y,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle v^{b,\varepsilon}(0,t)=v_{\ast}-v^{I,0}(1,t)+\Lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t),\ \ v^{b,\varepsilon}(-\infty,t)=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle v^{b,\varepsilon}(\xi,0)=0,\end{cases}

(2.17)

where
\Lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t)
is given by

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=-2\int_{0}^{t}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(1,s)+M)v^{I,0}(1,s)\int_{s}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad-2(\varphi_{0x}(1)+M)v_{\ast}\int_{0}^{t}\chi(\frac{\tau}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}})\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}\tau,

(2.18)

with
\chi
and
\alpha
being as in (
2.12
). Similar to (
2.14
) and (
2.16
), we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Big\|(v^{b,\varepsilon}-v^{b,0})\Big(\frac{x}{\varepsilon^{1/2}},t\Big)\Big\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L^{\infty}}+\Big\|\partial_{x}^{k}(\varphi^{b,\varepsilon}-\varphi^{b,1})\Big(\frac{x}{\varepsilon^{1/2}},t\Big)\Big\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L^{\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}

(2.19)

for
k=0,1
. We refer the reader to Appendix B for some properties of
\Lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t)
and Appendix
B
for the proof of (
2.16
) and (
2.19
).

Although we focus only on the convergence result for the leading-order profiles, some regularities and estimates of higher-order outer-/boundary-layer profiles are also needed in our analysis. These higher-order profiles will be constructed based on the approximate leading-order profile
(v^{B,\varepsilon},\varphi^{B,\varepsilon})
and
(v^{b,\varepsilon},\varphi^{b,\varepsilon})
. Precisely, the first-order outer-layer profile
(\varphi^{I,1},v^{I,1})
satisfies the following problem:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\varphi_{t}^{I,1}=\varphi_{xx}^{I,1}-(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}+M)v_{x}^{I,1}-\varphi_{x}^{I,1}v_{x}^{I,0},\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle v_{t}^{I,1}=-(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}+M)v^{I,1}-\varphi_{x}^{I,1}v^{I,0},\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle\varphi^{I,1}(0,t)=-\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}(0,t),\ \ \varphi^{I,1}(1,t)=-\varphi^{b,\varepsilon}(0,t),\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle(\varphi^{I,1},v^{I,1})(x,0)=(0,0).\end{cases}

(2.20)

With
(\varphi^{I,0},v^{I,0})
,
(\varphi^{I,1},v^{I,1})
and the leading-order boundary-layer profiles at hand, we continue to construct the boundary-layer profiles
\varphi^{B,2}
and
v^{B,1}
as follows

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle-\varphi_{zz}^{B,2}+v_{z}^{B,\varepsilon}(\varphi_{xx}^{I,0}(0,t)z+\varphi_{x}^{I,1}(0,t)+\varphi_{z}^{B,2})\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\quad+v_{z}^{B,1}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M+\varphi_{z}^{B,\varepsilon})+\varphi_{z}^{B,\varepsilon}v_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle v_{t}^{B,1}-v_{zz}^{B,1}+(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M)v^{B,1}+\varphi_{z}^{B,\varepsilon}(v_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)z+v^{I,1}(0,t)+v^{B,1})\\[5.69054pt]
\qquad+(\varphi_{xx}^{I,0}(0,t)z+\varphi_{x}^{I,1}(0,t))v^{B,\varepsilon}+\varphi_{z}^{B,2}(v^{I,0}(0,t)+v^{B,\varepsilon})=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle v^{B,1}(0,t)=-v^{I,1}(0,t),\ \ \varphi^{B,2}(+\infty,t)=v^{B,1}(+\infty,t)=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle(\varphi^{B,2},v^{B,1})(z,0)=(0,0).\end{cases}

(2.21)

Similarly,
(\varphi^{b,2},v^{b,1})
satisfies

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle-\varphi_{\xi\xi}^{b,2}+v_{\xi}^{b,\varepsilon}(\varphi_{xx}^{I,0}(1,t)\xi+\varphi_{x}^{I,1}(1,t)+\varphi_{\xi}^{b,2})\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\quad+v_{\xi}^{b,1}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(1,t)+M+\varphi_{\xi}^{b,\varepsilon})+\varphi_{\xi}^{b,\varepsilon}v_{x}^{I,0}(1,t)=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle v_{t}^{b,1}-v_{\xi\xi}^{b,1}+(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(1,t)+M)v^{b,1}+\varphi_{\xi}^{b,\varepsilon}(v_{x}^{I,0}(1,t)\xi+v^{I,1}(1,t)+v^{b,1})\\[5.69054pt]
\qquad+(\varphi_{xx}^{I,0}(1,t)\xi+\varphi_{x}^{I,1}(1,t))v^{b,\varepsilon}+\varphi_{\xi}^{b,2}(v^{I,0}(1,t)+v^{b,\varepsilon})=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle v^{b,1}(0,t)=-v^{I,1}(1,t),\ \ \varphi^{b,2}(-\infty,t)=v^{b,1}(-\infty,t)=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle(\varphi^{b,2},v^{b,1})(\xi,0)=(0,0).\end{cases}

(2.22)

Finally, we remark that the regularities of higher-order profiles always depend on the regularities of lower-order profiles. For example,
\varphi^{B,1}
is determined by
v^{B,0}
and the leading-order outer-layer profiles. We can obtain the existence and regularity of solutions to the problem (
2.20
) only if
\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}
and
\varphi^{b,\varepsilon}
are regular enough and satisfy certain compatibility conditions. We should emphasize that since we have approximated
v^{B,0}
and
v^{b,0}
by the
\varepsilon
-dependent corner-corrector functions
\Lambda_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t)\,(i=1,2)
and constructed the higher-order profiles based on
v^{B,\varepsilon}
and
v^{b,\varepsilon}
determined by (
2.11
) and (
2.17
), respectively, the dependence on
\varepsilon
of the norms of these profiles will play a crucial role in proving the convergence of the leading-order boundary-layer profiles. This needs very delicate and careful analysis. We shall show the details of proving the regularities of these profiles in Sec.
3
.

2.2.
Statement of main results

To prove the convergence of the boundary-layer profiles constructed in the last subsection, we require that the initial data satisfy some compatibility conditions at the boundary. Specifically we assume that
(\varphi_{0},v_{0})
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{i}\varphi^{I,0}|_{t=0}=0\ (i=1,2,3)

\displaystyle\text{on}\ \partial\mathcal{I},

(2.23a)

\displaystyle v_{0}=v_{\ast}

\displaystyle\text{on}\ \partial\mathcal{I},

(2.23b)

where
\partial_{t}^{i}\varphi^{I,0}|_{t=0}
can be inductively derived from the equations in (
2.6
) as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\varphi^{I,0}|_{t=0}:=\varphi_{0xx}-(\varphi_{0x}+M)v_{0x},\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi^{I,0}|_{t=0}:=(\partial_{t}\varphi^{I,0}|_{t=0})_{xx}+(\varphi_{0x}+M)((\varphi_{0x}+M)v_{0})_{x}-(\partial_{t}\varphi^{I,0}|_{t=0})_{x}v_{0x},\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}^{3}\varphi^{I,0}|_{t=0}:=(\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi^{I,0}|_{t=0})_{xx}-(\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi^{I,0}|_{t=0})_{x}v_{0x}+2(\partial_{t}\varphi^{I,0}|_{t=0})_{x}((\varphi_{0x}+M)v_{0})_{x}\\[5.69054pt]
\qquad\qquad\qquad+(\varphi_{0x}+M)((\partial_{t}\varphi^{I,0}|_{t=0})_{x}v_{0})_{x}-(\varphi_{0x}+M)((\varphi_{0x}+M)^{2}v_{0})_{x}.\end{cases}

(2.24)

We say that the initial value
\varphi^{I,0}|_{t=0}
of the problem (
2.6
) is compatible with boundary conditions up to order three if it fulfills (
2.23a
), while the initial values of problem (
2.7
) and (
2.9
) are compatible with boundary conditions at the zero-th order if (
2.23b
) holds (i.e. the initial values of leading-order boundary-layer profiles and boundary conditions are equal at the corner
(0,0)
).

To proceed, let
\nu\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
be a constant such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle 1+\nu>\alpha,

(2.25)

and denote

\displaystyle\displaystyle\iota_{0}=\min\left\{\frac{3}{4}-\nu,\frac{\alpha}{2},1+\frac{\nu-\alpha}{2},1-\frac{2}{3}\nu,\frac{5}{4}-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right\},

(2.26)

where the constant
\alpha
is given in (
2.13
). Our main results on the convergence of boundary layer solutions for the reformulated problem (
2.2
) are given below.

Theorem 2.1
.

Assume that
(\varphi_{0},v_{0})\in H^{7}\times H^{7}
and
(\sqrt{v_{0}})_{x}\in L^{2}
with
\varphi_{0x}+M>0
and
v_{0}\geq 0
satisfying (
2.23
). Then for any
T>0
, there exists a constant
\varepsilon_{T}>0
with
\displaystyle\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\varepsilon_{T}=0
such that if
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{T}
, the problem (
2.2
) admits a unique solution
(\varphi^{\varepsilon},v^{\varepsilon})\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}\times H^{2})
satisfying:

\displaystyle\|\varphi^{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)-\varphi^{I,0}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{3\iota_{0}}{2}-\frac{3}{8}},

(2.27)

\displaystyle t^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\varphi_{x}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)-\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(\cdot,t)-\big(\varphi_{z}^{B,1}(\cdot,t)+\varphi_{\xi}^{b,1}(\cdot,t)\big)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon^{2\iota_{0}-1},

(2.28)

\displaystyle\|v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)-v^{I,0}(\cdot,t)-\big(v^{B,0}(\cdot,t)+v^{b,0}(\cdot,t)\big)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon^{\iota_{0}-\frac{1}{4}},

(2.29)

with
z:=\frac{x}{\varepsilon^{1/2}},\ \ \xi:=\frac{x-1}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}
,
where
(u^{I,0},v^{I,0})
,
v^{B,0}
and
v^{b,0}
are solutions to the problems (
2.6
), (
2.7
) and (
2.9
), respectively;
\varphi^{I,1}
is determined by (
2.20
);
\varphi^{B,1}
and
\varphi^{b,1}
are given by (
2.8
) and (
2.10
), respectively;
C>0
is a constant depending on
T
but independent of
\varepsilon
.

Remark 2.1
.

From (
2.13
) and (
2.26
), it follows that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}<\iota_{0}<\frac{7}{12}.

(2.30)

Indeed by
0<\nu<1/4
and
1<\alpha<1+\nu
, we can easily get
1/2<\iota_{0}
. If
\iota_{0}\geq\frac{7}{12}
, the fact
3/4-\nu\geq\iota_{0}
gives
\nu\leq 1/6
while the fact
\frac{1+\nu}{2}>\frac{\alpha}{2}\geq\iota_{0}
yields
\nu>1/6
. Then a contradiction arises and thus
\iota_{0}\leq\frac{7}{12}
. In particular, if we take
\alpha=\frac{11}{10}
,
\nu=\frac{1}{5}
, then
\iota_{0}=\frac{11}{20}
.

Based on the transformation (
2.1
), we can transfer the results of Theorem
2.1
to the original problem (
1.3
). Precisely, let

\displaystyle\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}=\varphi_{x}^{\varepsilon}+M,\ \ u^{I,0}=\varphi_{x}^{I,0}+M,

(2.31)

with
\varphi^{\varepsilon}
and
\varphi^{I,0}
being the solutions to the problem (
2.2
) and the problem (
2.6
), respectively. Then
(u^{\varepsilon},v^{\varepsilon})
and
(u^{I,0},v^{I,0})
solve the problem (
1.3
) for
\varepsilon>0
and
\varepsilon=0
, respectively. Moreover, with (
2.8
) and (
2.10
), we have

\displaystyle u^{B,0}\left(z,t\right):=

\displaystyle\varphi_{z}^{B,1}(z,t)=(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M)({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,0}(z,t)}-1),

(2.32)

\displaystyle u^{b,0}\left(\xi,t\right):=

\displaystyle\varphi_{\xi}^{b,1}(\xi,t)=(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(1,t)+M)({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{b,0}(\xi,t)}-1).

Then the results on the original problem are stated in the following.

Theorem 2.2
.

Assume that
(u_{0},v_{0})\in H^{6}\times H^{7}
and
(\sqrt{v_{0}})_{x}\in L^{2}
satisfying
\min_{x\in\bar{\mathcal{I}}}u_{0}>0
and (
2.23
) with
\varphi_{0}=\int_{0}^{x}(u_{0}-M)\mathrm{d}y
and
M=\int_{\mathcal{I}}u_{0}\mathrm{d}x
. Then for any
T>0
, there exists a constant
\varepsilon_{T}>0
with
\displaystyle\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\varepsilon_{T}=0
such that if
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{T}
, the problem (
1.3
)-(
1.4
) admits a unique solution
(u^{\varepsilon},v^{\varepsilon})\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}\times H^{2})
on
[0,T]
, which satisfies

\displaystyle t^{\frac{5}{4}}\|u^{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)-u^{I,0}(\cdot,t)-\big(v^{B,0}(\cdot,t)+u^{b,0}(\cdot,t)\big)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon^{2\iota_{0}-1},

(2.33)

\displaystyle\|v^{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)-v^{I,0}(\cdot,t)-\big(v^{B,0}(\cdot,t)+v^{b,0}(\cdot,t)\big)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon^{\iota_{0}-\frac{1}{4}},

where
z:=\frac{x}{\varepsilon^{1/2}},\ \ \xi:=\frac{x-1}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}
, and
C>0
is a constant depending on
T
but independent of
\varepsilon
. Here
(u^{I,0},v^{I,0})(x,t)
,
v^{B,0}(z,t)
and
v^{b,0}(\xi,t)
are solutions to the problems (
2.6
), (
2.7
) and (
2.9
), respectively;
u^{B,0}(z,t)
and
u^{b,0}(\xi,t)
are given in (
2.32
).

Remark 2.2
.

From (
2.33
), for any
v_{*}>0
, we get that

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon}(x,t)-u^{I,0}(x,t)-(u^{B,0}(z,t)+u^{b,0}(\xi,t))\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon^{2\iota_{0}-1},\ \forall\ 0<t\leq T

where
T>0
is independent of
v_{*}
and can be arbitrarily large. That is, the convergence of the boundary-layer profile
u^{\varepsilon}
holds for any
t>0
while
v^{\varepsilon}
for any
t\geq 0
. However, the convergence result in
[
5
]
with degenerate initial data
u_{0}
satisfying
u_{0}|_{\partial\mathcal{I}}=0
holds only for some finite time
T_{0}(v_{*})
depending on
v_{*}
. This is the major difference between degenerate and non-degenerate initial data
u_{0}
.

3.
Regularity of the outer-/boundary-layer profiles

In this section, we shall derive the requisite regularities of solutions to problems (
2.6
), (
2.11
), (
2.17
), (
2.20
), (
2.21
), (
2.22
), respectively. The proofs are based on the energy method along with some basic mathematical tools introduced in Appendix
A
.
We start by showing the following estimates on the corner-corrector functions
\Lambda_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t),i=1,2
.

Lemma 3.1
.

It holds for
i=1,2
and any
\lambda\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-\alpha}\|\partial_{t}^{j}\Lambda_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}((0,T))}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\Lambda_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{1}((0,T))}+\|(t^{\frac{2k-3}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha(2k-3)}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\Lambda_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}((0,T))}^{2}\leq C,

(3.1)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|t^{\frac{2k-3+\lambda}{2}}\partial_{t}^{k}\Lambda_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}((0,T))}^{2}+\|t^{\lambda}\partial_{t}\Lambda_{i}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T))}\leq C\varepsilon^{\alpha\lambda},

(3.2)

where
j=0,1
,
k=2,3
,
C>0
is a constant independent of
\varepsilon
.

Proof.

We shall prove the estimates in (
3.1
) and (
3.2
) for
\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)
only, and the estimates for
\Lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t)
can be obtained in the same manner. Recalling (
2.12
), we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=-2\int_{0}^{t}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,s)+M)v^{I,0}(0,s)\int_{s}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad-2(\varphi_{0x}(0)+M)v_{\ast}\int_{0}^{t}\chi(\frac{\tau}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}})\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.3)

It follows from direct computation that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,s)+M)v^{I,0}(0,s)\int_{s}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle~\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,s)+M)v^{I,0}(0,s)\int_{\frac{s}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}}}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\tau^{2}}}{\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}s\leq C\varepsilon^{\alpha}\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}}}\int_{\lambda}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\tau^{2}}}{\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\lambda

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\alpha}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\lambda}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\tau^{2}}}{\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\lambda\leq C\varepsilon^{\alpha},

(3.4)

where we have used Corollary
3.1
and (
A.2
), and the constant
C>0
is independent of
\varepsilon
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\chi(\frac{\tau}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}})\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C\varepsilon^{\alpha},

(3.5)

where
C>0
is a constant independent of
\varepsilon
. Therefore we get
\|\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T))}\leq C\varepsilon^{\alpha}
for some constant
C>0
which may depend on
T
but independent of
\varepsilon
. Differentiating
\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)
with respect to
t
leads to

\displaystyle\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=-2(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M)v^{I,0}(0,t)\int_{t}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad-2(\varphi_{0x}(0)+M)v_{\ast}\chi(\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}})\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma.

(3.6)

This along with (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) gives

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}((0,T))}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{\alpha}.

Differentiating (
3.6
) with respect to
t
, we get, thanks to Corollary
3.1
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{2}\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)\sim\int_{t}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma+\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{t^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}}\chi^{\prime}(\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}})\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{4\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma+\chi(\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}})\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{t^{2}}{4\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}.

Then it follows from direct computation that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{2}\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{1}((0,T))}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{t}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{t^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}}\chi^{\prime}(\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}})\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{4\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\chi(\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}})\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{t^{2}}{4\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}t\leq C

and

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|(t^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})[\partial_{t}^{2}\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}((0,T))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}(t+\varepsilon^{\alpha})\int_{t}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}t+C\int_{0}^{T}(t+\varepsilon^{\alpha})\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{t^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}\pi}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{0}^{T}(t+\varepsilon^{\alpha})\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}[\chi^{\prime}(\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}})]^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{4\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{0}^{T}(t+\varepsilon^{\alpha})\chi(\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}})\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{t^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}\pi}\mathrm{d}t\leq C,

where the constant
C>0
is independent of
\varepsilon
. Similar arguments further imply for any
\lambda>0
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|(t^{\frac{1+\lambda}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha+\lambda}{2}})\partial_{t}^{2}\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}((0,T))}^{2}+\|(t^{\frac{3+\lambda}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha+\lambda}{2}})\partial_{t}^{3}\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}((0,T))}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{\lambda\alpha}.

Finally, by (
3.6
), the basic fact
t^{\lambda}\int_{t}^{\infty}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\tau^{2}}\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C_{\lambda}
for some
C_{\lambda}>0
depending on
\lambda
and the fact that
\chi
is bounded and compactly supported, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|t^{\lambda}\partial_{t}\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{\lambda}\int_{t}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma+C\chi(\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}})t^{\lambda}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}}}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\leq C

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\alpha\lambda}\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}}\right)^{\lambda}\left[\int_{\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}}}^{\infty}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\sigma^{2}}\mathrm{d}\sigma+\chi(\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}})\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{-\sigma^{2}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\right]\leq C\varepsilon^{\alpha\lambda}.

This finishes the proof of Lemma
3.1
.
∎

Next we proceed with the problem on the leading-order outer-layer profile
(\varphi^{I,0},v^{I,0})
. In
[
5
]
, we prove the global existence and uniqueness of classical solutions to the problem (
2.2
). We quote the result here for later use.

Lemma 3.2
.

Assume that
(\varphi_{0},v_{0})\in H^{7}\times H^{7}
and
(\sqrt{v_{0}})_{x}\in L^{2}
satisfying (
2.23
) and
\min_{x\in\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(\varphi_{0x}+M)>0
. Then for any
T>0
, there exists a unique solution
(\varphi^{I,0},v^{I,0})
to the problem (
2.6
) on
[0,T]
satisfying

\displaystyle K^{-1}\leq\varphi_{x}^{I,0}+M\leq K,\ \ \ \partial_{t}^{k}\varphi^{I,0}\in L_{T}^{2}H^{8-2k}\ \ \mbox{for }k=0,1,2,3,4,

(3.7a)

\displaystyle\displaystyle v^{I,0}\in

\displaystyle L_{T}^{\infty}H^{7},\ \ \partial_{t}^{k}v^{I,0}\in L_{T}^{2}H^{9-2k}\ \ \mbox{for }k=1,2,3,4,

(3.7b)

where
K>0
is a constant.

Using Proposition
A.1
in Appendix A and Lemma
3.2
, we obtain the following estimates on
(\varphi^{I,0},v^{I,0})
.

Corollary 3.1
.

Under the conditions of Lemma
3.2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\|\varphi^{I,0}\right\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H^{7}}+\|v^{I,0}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H^{7}}+\|\partial_{t}^{k}\varphi^{I,0}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H^{7-2k}}+\|\partial_{t}^{k}v^{I,0}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H^{8-2k}}\leq C\ \ \ k=1,2,3.

(3.8)

Remark 3.1
.

As mentioned before, the problem (
2.6
) is exactly the zero-diffusion problem of (
2.2
), which is equivalent to the zero-diffusion problem of (
1.3
) in the sense of classical solutions. Precisely, let
(\varphi^{I,0},v^{I,0})
be the solution obtained in Lemma
3.2
and denote
u^{I,0}=\varphi_{x}^{I,0}+M
. Then
(u^{I,0},v^{I,0})
is the unique classical solution to the zero-diffusion problem of (
1.3
).

Next lemma presents the regularity of the boundary-layer profiles
v^{B,\varepsilon}
and
\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
(\varphi^{I,0},v^{I,0})
be the solution to the problem (
2.6
) obtained in Lemma
3.2
. Then for any
T>0
, there exists a positive constant
\hat{\varepsilon}_{T}
such that if
\varepsilon\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{T}
, the problem (
2.11
) admits a unique solution
v^{B,\varepsilon}
on
[0,T]
such that for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{t}^{k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\in L_{T}^{2}H_{z}^{6-2k}\,(k=0,1,2,3)
and

\displaystyle\displaystyle 0\leq v^{B,\varepsilon}\leq v_{\ast},\ \ \ \|\langle z\rangle^{l}v_{t}^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{2}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{t}^{k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{z}^{3-2k}}\leq C\ \ \mbox{for }k=0,1,

(3.9a)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{i}\partial_{z}^{4-2i}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{3}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{i}\partial_{z}^{5-2i}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}\leq C\ \ \mbox{for }i=0,1,2,

(3.9b)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{6-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}\leq C\ \ \mbox{for }k=0,1,2,3.

(3.9c)

Furthermore, for
\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}
defined in (
2.15
), it holds that
\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{t}^{k}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\in L_{T}^{2}H^{7-2k}\,(k=0,1,2,3)
with

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\varphi_{t}^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H_{z}^{1}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{t}^{k}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{z}^{4-2k}}\leq C\ \mbox{for }\,k=0,1,

(3.10a)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{i}\partial_{z}^{k}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}\leq C\ \mbox{for }\,2i+k=5,

(3.10b)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{3}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{6-2k}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}\leq C\ \ \mbox{for }\,k=0,1,2,

(3.10c)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{3}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{7-2k}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}\leq C\ \ \mbox{for }\,k=0,1,2,3,

(3.10d)

where
\alpha
is as in (
2.12
),
C>0
is a constant depending on
T
, but independent of
\varepsilon
.

Before proving Lemma
3.3
, we extract some important facts resulting from Lemma
3.3
and Proposition
A.1
in the following corollary.

Corollary 3.2
.

Under the conditions of Lemma
3.3
, it holds for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
k=0,1
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{t}^{k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H_{z}^{2-2k}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{3-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{2}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{2}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H_{z}^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{3}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{4-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{2}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{5-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{2}}\leq C,

(3.11)

and that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{t}^{k}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H_{z}^{3-2k}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{4-2k}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{2}}\leq C,

(3.12a)

\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H_{z}^{2}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{3}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{5-2k}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{2}}\leq C,

(3.12b)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{6-2k}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{2}}\leq C,

(3.12c)

where
C>0
is a constant depending on
T
, but independent of
\varepsilon
.

Proof of Lemma
3.3
.

Given any
\varepsilon>0
, the global existence and uniqueness of solutions to the problem (
2.11
) with regularity as in (
3.9
) can be proved by similar arguments as
[
5
]
. Here we mainly focus on the
a priori
estimates of the solution on
[0,T]
for any
T>0
, as well as the dependence of the norms of solutions on
\varepsilon
which are of importance in the study on the convergence of boundary-layer profiles in the sequel. We first prove the bounds of the solution, i.e.,

\displaystyle\displaystyle 0\leq v^{B,\varepsilon}\leq v_{\ast}.

(3.13)

Recalling
\varphi_{x}^{I,0}+M\geq K^{-1}>0
from (
3.7a
), the estimate of
\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)
in (
3.1
) and the fact

\displaystyle\displaystyle v^{I,0}(0,t)=v_{\ast}\mathop{\mathrm{exp}}\nolimits\left(-\int_{0}^{t}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M)\mathrm{d}\tau\right),

(3.14)

we conclude that there exists a positive constant
\varepsilon_{1}
(may depend on
T
) such that for any
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{1}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\displaystyle v_{\ast}\geq v_{\ast}-v^{I,0}(0,t)+\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)>0.

Then testing the equation (
2.11
) against
v^{-}:=-\max\{0,-v^{B,\varepsilon}\}
, we derive that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left|v^{-}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left|\partial_{z}v^{-}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M){\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}\left|v^{-}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\{v^{B,\varepsilon}<0\}}(\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M)\,v^{I,0}(0,t)({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}-1)v^{B,\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}z=0,

This entails that
\|v^{-}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{2}}\leq 0
. Then we get
v^{B,\varepsilon}\geq 0
. Similarly, testing the equation (
2.7
) against
v^{+}:=\max\{v^{B,\varepsilon}-v_{\ast},0\}
, we have
v^{B,\varepsilon}\leq v_{\ast}
. Therefore (
3.13
) is proved.

To proceed, let
\eta(z)\in C^{\infty}([0,\infty))
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\eta(0)=1,\ \ \eta(z)=0\ \ \mbox{for}\ z\geq 1,

(3.15)

and denote by
\overline{u^{I,0}}:=\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M
,
\overline{v^{I,0}}:=v^{I,0}(0,t)
. Furthermore, take
\vartheta=v^{B,\varepsilon}-\eta(z)(v_{\ast}-\overline{v^{I,0}}+\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t))=:v^{B,\varepsilon}-\phi(z,t)
. Then
\vartheta
solves

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\vartheta_{t}=\vartheta_{zz}-\overline{u^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}(\vartheta+\phi)-\overline{u^{I,0}}\,\overline{v^{I,0}}({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}-1)+\varrho,\\
\displaystyle\vartheta(0,t)=0,\ \ \vartheta(+\infty,t)=0,\\
\displaystyle\vartheta(z,0)=0,\end{cases}

(3.16)

where

\displaystyle\displaystyle\varrho=\eta_{zz}(z)(v_{\ast}-\overline{v^{I,0}}+\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t))-\eta(z)(v_{\ast}-\overline{v^{I,0}}+\Lambda_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t))_{t}.

In view of (
3.7
), (
3.8
) and (
3.1
), it holds for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{t}^{i}\phi\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H_{z}^{5}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{2k-1}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha(2k-1)}{2}})\partial_{t}^{1+k}\phi\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{z}^{6-2k}}\leq C,\ \ i=0,1,\,k=1,2,

(3.17a)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\varrho\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H_{z}^{4}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{t}\varrho\|_{L_{T}^{1}H_{z}^{2}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{2k-1}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha(2k-1)}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\varrho\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{z}^{4-2k}}\leq C,\ \ k=1,2,

(3.17b)

where
C>0
is a constant independent of
\varepsilon
. Multiplying the equation
\eqref{eq-for-esti-v-B-0}_{1}
by
\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta
followed by an integration over
\mathbb{R}_{+}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta^{2}\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta_{z}^{2}\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\overline{u^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}\vartheta^{2}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta\varrho\mathrm{d}z-2l\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l-2}z\vartheta_{z}\vartheta\mathrm{d}z-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\overline{u^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}\vartheta\phi\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\overline{u^{I,0}}\,\overline{v^{I,0}}\left({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}-1\right)\vartheta\mathrm{d}z=:\Lambda,

(3.18)

where, due to
\overline{u^{I,0}}=\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)+M\geq K^{-1}>0
from (
3.7a
) and
0\leq v^{B,\varepsilon}\leq v_{\ast}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\overline{u^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}\vartheta^{2}\mathrm{d}z\geq C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta^{2}\mathrm{d}z,

(3.19)

where
C
is a positive constant depending on
T
.
We now estimate the terms on the right hand side of (
3.18
). By (
3.7
),
0\leq v^{B,\varepsilon}\leq v_{\ast}
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we get

\displaystyle\Lambda

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\vartheta\|_{L_{z}^{2}}\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\varrho\|_{L_{z}^{2}}+C\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\vartheta\|_{L_{z}^{2}}\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\vartheta_{z}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}+C\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\vartheta\|_{L_{z}^{2}}\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\phi\|_{L_{z}^{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\overline{u^{I,0}}\,\overline{v^{I,0}}\left(\left|\vartheta\right|+\left|\phi\right|\right)\vartheta\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta_{z}^{2}\mathrm{d}z+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta^{2}\mathrm{d}z+C\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\varrho\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}+C\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\phi\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}.

(3.20)

Inserting (
3.19
) and (
3.20
) into (
3.18
), we get after integrating the resulting inequality over
(0,t)
for any
t\in(0,T]
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta^{2}(\cdot,t)\mathrm{d}z+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left(\vartheta^{2}+\vartheta_{z}^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta^{2}\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.21)

Applying the Gronwall inequality to (
3.21
), it follows that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta^{2}(\cdot,t)\mathrm{d}z+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left(\vartheta^{2}+\vartheta_{z}^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C.

(3.22)

Testing
\eqref{eq-for-esti-v-B-0}_{1}
against
\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta_{t}
, we get

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left(\vartheta_{z}^{2}+\overline{u^{I,0}}\vartheta^{2}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}\right)\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta_{t}^{2}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle~\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\partial_{t}\overline{u^{I,0}}\vartheta^{2}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}\mathrm{d}z+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\overline{u^{I,0}}\vartheta^{2}\left(\vartheta_{t}+\phi_{t}\right){\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}\mathrm{d}z-2l\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l-2}z\vartheta_{t}\vartheta_{z}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\overline{u^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}\phi\vartheta_{t}\mathrm{d}z-\overline{u^{I,0}}\,\overline{v^{I,0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}-1)\vartheta_{t}\mathrm{d}z-\int_{\mathcal{I}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\varrho\vartheta_{t}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\leq C|\partial_{t}\overline{u^{I,0}}|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta^{2}\mathrm{d}z+C|\overline{u^{I,0}}|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta^{2}(\left|\vartheta_{t}\right|+\left|\phi_{t}\right|)\mathrm{d}z+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l-1}\left|\vartheta_{t}\right|\left|\vartheta_{z}\right|\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\displaystyle~\quad+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left|\phi\right|\left|\vartheta_{t}\right|\mathrm{d}z+C|\overline{u^{I,0}}||\overline{v^{I,0}}|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left(\left|\vartheta\right|+\left|\phi\right|\right)\left|\vartheta_{t}\right|\mathrm{d}z-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\varrho\vartheta_{t}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta_{t}^{2}\mathrm{d}z+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta_{z}^{2}\mathrm{d}z+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}(\phi^{2}+\phi_{t}^{2})\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\varrho^{2}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\quad+C(|\partial_{t}\overline{u^{I,0}}|+|\overline{u^{I,0}}|^{2}+|\overline{u^{I,0}}|^{2}|\overline{v^{I,0}}|^{2})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta^{2}\mathrm{d}z,

(3.23)

where we have used (
3.7
), (
3.22
),
0\leq v^{B,\varepsilon}\leq v_{\ast}
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. By (
3.7
) and the Sobolev inequality (
A.2
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)\|_{L^{2}(0,T)}\leq C\|\partial_{t}^{k}\varphi_{x}^{I,0}\|_{L_{T}^{2}H^{1}}\leq C\ \ \mbox{for}\ \ 0\leq k\leq 3,\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}v^{I,0}(0,t)\|_{L^{2}(0,T)}\leq C\|\partial_{t}^{k}v^{I,0}\|_{L_{T}^{2}H^{1}}\leq C\ \ \mbox{for}\ \ 0\leq k\leq 4,\end{cases}

which gives rise to

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}\varphi_{x}^{I,0}(0,t)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T)}\leq C\ \ \mbox{for}\ 0\leq k\leq 2\ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \|\partial_{t}^{k}v^{I,0}(0,t)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T)}\leq C\ \ \mbox{for}\ \ 0\leq k\leq 3.

(3.24)

With (
3.24
), we update (
3.23
) as

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left(\vartheta_{z}^{2}+\overline{u^{I,0}}\vartheta^{2}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}\right)\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta_{t}^{2}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left(\vartheta^{2}+\vartheta_{z}^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}z+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}(\phi^{2}+\phi_{t}^{2})\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\varrho^{2}\mathrm{d}z.

This along with (
3.7
), (
3.17
), (
3.19
) and the Gronwall inequality yields for any
t\in(0,T]
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left(\vartheta^{2}+\vartheta_{z}^{2}\right)(\cdot,t)\mathrm{d}z+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta_{\tau}^{2}\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C.

(3.25)

By (
3.7
), (
3.17
) and (
3.25
), we further get from
\eqref{eq-for-esti-v-B-0}_{1}
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta_{zz}^{2}\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}t\leq C.

(3.26)

Denote by
\tilde{\vartheta}:=\vartheta_{t}
. Then by the compatibility condition (
2.24
),
\tilde{\vartheta}
satisfies

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\tilde{\vartheta}_{t}=\tilde{\vartheta}_{zz}-\overline{u^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}\tilde{\vartheta}-\overline{u^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}(\vartheta+\phi)\tilde{\vartheta}-\overline{u^{I,0}}\,\overline{v^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}\tilde{\vartheta}+\tilde{\varrho},\\
\displaystyle\tilde{\vartheta}(0,t)=0,\ \ \tilde{\vartheta}(+\infty,t)=0,\\
\displaystyle\tilde{\vartheta}(z,0)=0,\end{cases}

(3.27)

where
\tilde{\varrho}
is given by

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tilde{\varrho}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{t}\overline{u^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}(\vartheta+\phi)-\overline{u^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}\phi_{t}(1+\vartheta+\phi)-\overline{u^{I,0}}\,v^{I,0}(0,t){\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}\phi_{t}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad-\partial_{t}\left(\overline{u^{I,0}}\,\overline{v^{I,0}}\right)({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}-1)+\partial_{t}\varrho.

By (
3.7
), (
3.17
), (
3.22
),
(
3.24
)–(
3.26
) and (
3.1
), it holds for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\tilde{\varrho}\|_{L_{T}^{1}H_{z}^{2}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{2k+1}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha(2k+1)}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\tilde{\varrho}\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{z}^{2-2k}}\leq C\ \ \mbox{for }k=0,1.

(3.28)

Testing the equation in (
3.27
) against
\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}
, using integration by parts, the Hölder inequality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}^{2}\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}^{2}\left(\overline{u^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}+\overline{u^{I,0}}\,\overline{v^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}+\overline{u^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}(\vartheta+\phi)\right)\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{z}^{2}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle~\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}\tilde{\varrho}\mathrm{d}z-2l\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l-2}z\tilde{\vartheta}_{z}\tilde{\vartheta}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{z}^{2}\mathrm{d}z+C(1+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\tilde{\varrho}\|_{L_{z}^{2}})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}^{2}\mathrm{d}z+C\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\tilde{\varrho}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}.

That is,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}^{2}\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{z}^{2}\mathrm{d}z\leq C(1+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\tilde{\varrho}\|_{L_{z}^{2}})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}^{2}\mathrm{d}z+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\tilde{\varrho}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}.

(3.29)

Then applying the Gronwall inequality to (
3.29
), by virtue of (
3.28
), one has

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}^{2}(\cdot,t)\mathrm{d}z+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{z}^{2}\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C

(3.30)

for any
t\in(0,T]
, where
C>0
is a constant independent of
\varepsilon
. Furthermore, by (
3.17
), (
3.25
), the equation in (
3.16
) and the bounds of
v^{B,\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\vartheta_{zz}(\cdot,t)\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\vartheta_{zzz}^{2}\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C.

(3.31)

Gathering (
3.22
), (
3.25
), (
3.26
), (
3.30
) and (
3.31
), we get for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
and any
t\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\vartheta(\cdot,t)\|_{H_{z}^{2}}^{2}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\vartheta_{t}(\cdot,t)\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left(|\vartheta_{\tau z}|^{2}+|\vartheta_{zzz}|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C,

(3.32)

where
C>0
is a constant independent of
\varepsilon
. This along with the definition of
\vartheta
gives

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}v^{B,\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)\|_{H_{z}^{2}}^{2}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}v_{t}^{B,\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left(|v_{\tau z}^{B,\varepsilon}|^{2}+|v_{zzz}^{B,\varepsilon}|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C.

(3.33)

Now let us turn to some higher-order estimates on
v^{B,\varepsilon}
which, similar to the higher-order estimates on
\tilde{\varrho}
in (
3.28
), may be time-weighted or
\varepsilon
-dependent. Precisely, multiplying the equation in (
3.27
) by
\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{t}
followed by an integration over
\mathbb{R}_{+}
, one deduces that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{z}^{2}\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{t}^{2}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle~\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}\tilde{\vartheta}_{t}\left(\overline{u^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}+\overline{u^{I,0}}\,\overline{v^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}+\overline{u^{I,0}}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{\vartheta+\phi}(\vartheta+\phi)\right)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\displaystyle~\quad-2l\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l-2}z\tilde{\vartheta}_{z}\tilde{\vartheta}_{t}\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{t}\tilde{\varrho}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{t}^{2}\mathrm{d}z+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left(\tilde{\vartheta}^{2}+\tilde{\vartheta}_{z}^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}z+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\varrho}^{2}\mathrm{d}z,

(3.34)

where we have used (
3.24
), the bounds of
\phi
and
v^{B,\varepsilon}
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Noting from (
3.28
) that
\varepsilon^{\alpha(2k+1)/2}\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{t}^{k}\tilde{\varrho}\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{z}^{2-2k}}\leq C\,(k=0,1)
, we thus get after integrating (
3.34
) over
(0,t)
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{z}^{2}(\cdot,t)\mathrm{d}z+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{\tau}^{2}\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\tau\leq Cv_{\ast}^{2}\varepsilon^{-\alpha},

where (
3.30
) has been used, and the constant
C>0
is independent of
\varepsilon
. This along with
\eqref{eq-for-esti-v-B-0}_{1}
,
\eqref{tild-vte-eq}_{1}
and (
3.33
) implies that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left(|\vartheta_{tz}|^{2}+|\vartheta_{zzz}|^{2}\right)(\cdot,t)\mathrm{d}z+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left(|\vartheta_{\tau\tau}|^{2}+|\partial_{z}^{4}\vartheta|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C\varepsilon^{-\alpha}.

(3.35)

On the other hand, multiplying (
3.34
) by
t
, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\{t\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{z}^{2}\mathrm{d}z\right\}+t\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{t}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}\leq\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{z}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}+Ct\left(\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\tilde{\vartheta}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{z}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}\right)+t\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\tilde{\rho}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2},

which along with (
3.28
) and (
3.30
) immediately gives

\displaystyle\displaystyle t\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{z}(\cdot,t)\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\tau\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\tilde{\vartheta}_{\tau}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C,

where the constant
C>0
is independent of
\varepsilon
. By analogous arguments as proving (
3.35
), we further get

\displaystyle\displaystyle\displaystyle t\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\langle z\rangle^{2l}\left(|\vartheta_{tz}|^{2}+|\vartheta_{zzz}|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}z+\int_{0}^{t}\tau\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\langle z\rangle^{2l}|\vartheta_{\tau\tau}|^{2}+\langle z\rangle^{2l}|\partial_{z}^{4}\vartheta|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C.

Before proceeding, we would like to remark that for the problem (
3.16
), we have estimates for
\|\partial_{t}^{k}\vartheta\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{z}^{4-2k}}\,(k=0,1,2)
provided
\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{t}^{k}\varrho\in L_{T}^{2}H_{z}^{2-2k}\,(k=0,1)
, and that the dependence of the norms of
\partial_{t}^{k}\vartheta
on
\varepsilon
is mainly determined by
\varrho
. Notice that the initial data for the problem (
3.27
) are compatible up to order one, and that
\partial_{t}^{k}\tilde{\varrho}\,(k=0,1)
possess the estimates in (
3.28
). Therefore, by similar arguments as proving estimates for the problem (
3.16
), we have for the problem (
3.27
) that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|(t^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\tilde{\vartheta}\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{z}^{2-2k}}^{2}+\|(t^{\frac{3}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{3-2k}\tilde{\vartheta}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}^{2}+\|(t^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{j}\partial_{z}^{4-2j}\tilde{\vartheta}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}^{2}\leq C,

(3.36)

where
k=0,1
,
j=0,1,2
. We should point out that the index of the power of the time weight function in the norm of
\partial_{t}^{j}\partial_{z}^{4-2j}\tilde{\vartheta}
is larger than that in the norm of
\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{3-2k}\tilde{\vartheta}
. This is because the former is derived based on the latter (need to control
\|\langle z\rangle^{l}t^{2-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}\partial_{z}\tilde{\vartheta}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}
). Combining (
3.36
) with (
3.16
), (
3.17
), (
3.32
) and (
3.35
) gives further that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{3+k}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{z}^{5+k}\vartheta\|_{L_{T}^{2}L^{2}}\leq C,\ \ k=0,1.

(3.37)

Collecting (
3.33
), (
3.36
)–(
3.37
), recalling (
3.17
) and the definition of
\vartheta
, we then get (
3.9a
)–(
3.9c
).

To derive estimates for
\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}
, we need some more delicate estimates on
v^{B,\varepsilon}
. By (
3.9
) and Proposition
A.1
, we get for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
k=0,1
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{t}^{k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H_{z}^{2-2k}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{3-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{2}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{2}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H_{z}^{1}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{3}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{4-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{2}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}})\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{5-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{2}}\leq C,

(3.38)

where we have used the following inequality for
k=0,1
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle t\|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{3-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\left(\|\partial_{\tau}^{k}\partial_{z}^{3-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}+2\tau\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\partial_{\tau}^{k}\partial_{z}^{3-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\partial_{\tau}^{k+1}\partial_{z}^{3-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}z\right)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\left(\|\partial_{\tau}^{k}\partial_{z}^{3-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}+\tau^{2}\|\partial_{\tau}^{k+1}\partial_{z}^{3-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C.

Furthermore, in view of the Sobolev inequality (
A.4
), we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{z}^{i}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{\infty}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t^{\frac{1}{4}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha}{4}})\partial_{t}^{i}\partial_{z}^{2-2i}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{\infty}}+\|t^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}(t+\varepsilon^{\alpha})\partial_{t}^{i}\partial_{z}^{3-2i}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{\infty}}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}t^{\frac{7}{4}}\partial_{t}^{i}\partial_{z}^{4-2i}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{\infty}}\leq C

(3.39)

for
i=0,1
, and it holds for
k=0,1,2
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}}\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{z}^{4-2k}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{\infty}}\leq C.

Now we are ready to estimate
\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}
. Since

\displaystyle\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{\ell}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}=\sum_{\ell_{1}+\cdots+\ell_{r}=\ell\atop 1\leq\ell_{1}\leq\cdots\leq\ell_{r},\,1\leq r\leq\ell}C_{r}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}\partial_{z}^{\ell_{1}}v^{B,\varepsilon}\cdots\partial_{z}^{\ell_{r}}v^{B,\varepsilon},\ \ \ell\geq 1

for some constant
C_{r}
, then we get thanks to (
3.9
), (
3.38
) and (
3.39
) that for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{t}^{i}({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}-1)\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H_{z}^{2-2i}}\leq C,\ \ i=0,1,

(3.40)

and that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\|\langle z\rangle^{l}({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}-1)\right\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{z}^{3}}^{2}

\displaystyle\displaystyle~\leq\sum_{\ell_{1}+\cdots+\ell_{r}\leq 3\atop 1\leq\ell_{1}\leq\cdots\leq\ell_{r},\,1\leq r\leq 3}C_{r}\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{z}^{\ell_{1}}v^{B,\varepsilon}\cdots\partial_{z}^{\ell_{r}}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}^{2}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}-1)\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\displaystyle~\leq\sum_{\ell_{1}+\cdots+\ell_{r}\leq 3\atop 1\leq\ell_{1}\leq\cdots\leq\ell_{r},\,1\leq r\leq 3}\makebox[-4.0pt]{~}C\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{z}^{\ell_{1}}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{z}^{\infty}}^{2}\cdots\|\partial_{z}^{\ell_{r-1}}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{z}^{\infty}}^{2}\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{z}^{\ell_{r}}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{z}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t+C\|\langle z\rangle^{l}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{z}^{\ell}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}^{2}+\|\langle z\rangle^{l}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\|\langle z\rangle^{l}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{z}^{3}}^{2}\leq C.

(3.41)

This along with (
2.15
) gives
\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}H_{z}^{4}}\leq C
. Noting
\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{z}^{i}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{\infty}}\leq C~(i=0,1)
from (
3.39
) and

\displaystyle\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{4}({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}-1)=\sum_{\ell_{1}+\cdots+\ell_{r}=4\atop 1\leq\ell_{1}\leq\cdots\leq\ell_{r}\leq 3,\,1\leq r\leq 4}C_{r}{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}\partial_{z}^{\ell_{1}}v^{B,\varepsilon}\cdots\partial_{z}^{\ell_{r}}v^{B,\varepsilon}+{\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}\partial_{z}^{4}v^{B,\varepsilon},

by analogous arguments as in (
3.41
), we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{z}^{4}({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}-1)\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\ell=1}^{4}\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{z}^{\ell}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}^{2}+C\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{z}^{2}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{\infty}}^{2}\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{z}^{2}v^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{z}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-\alpha},

where we have used (
3.9
) and (
3.11
). Therefore we arrive at
\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{z}^{5}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}\leq C\varepsilon^{-\alpha/2}
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{z}^{i}({\mathop{\mathrm{e}}}^{v^{B,\varepsilon}}-1)\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}\leq C\varepsilon^{-\frac{3\alpha}{2}}\mbox{ for }i=5,6,

and thus
\|\langle z\rangle^{l}\partial_{z}^{k}\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{2}L_{z}^{2}}\leq C\varepsilon^{-\frac{3\alpha}{2}}
for
k=6,7
. Next we shall estimate the time derivatives of
\varphi^{B,\varepsilon}
. First, we get from (
2.15
), (
3.8
) and (
3.40
) that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\varphi_{t}^{B,\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}H_{z}^{1}}\leq C.

(3.42)

By (
3.9a
) and (
3.11
), it holds that
